# Schematic review: Sunbeam EM7000 heater wall board

This record holds every round of the schematic review of `heater-wall-board.kicad_sch`, as `docs/board-design-method.md` (section "Schematic review") requires.

## Round 1

- **Schematic judged**: git commit e6a1570234d2e76452e6fbc4bb8f5610a44de14b (hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board/heater-wall-board.kicad_sch and its four sheets 100, 200, 300, 400). The board's files are as in the commit; the schematic is unchanged since commit a5e7e5e5.
- **Netlist**: `/Applications/KiCad/KiCad.app/Contents/MacOS/kicad-cli sch export netlist --output /tmp/wall-review/work/review.net hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board/heater-wall-board.kicad_sch` (KiCad 10.0.7; 14 parts, 16 nets of which 4 are the `unconnected-` no-connect nets of U101 and U201, 5 sheets including the root).
- **Records read**: hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board/pin-allocation.yaml, parts-specification.yaml, catalogue-extract.yaml, derating-policy.yaml, pin-limits.yaml, board-rules.yaml, manufacturer-limits.yaml, circuit-proof.md, circuit-verdicts.md, bring-up-provision.md, open-items.md, fabrication-notes.md, constraints-figures.md, schematic-spec.yaml; hardware/sunbeam-em7000/separation-basis.yaml (accepted_on 2026-11-18); extracts of docs/sunbeam-em7000/controller-board-division.md (the path of the heater current, snubber row, D7, D15), controller-board-design-constraints.md (enclosure air, pending marks, heater switches, mains entry), and the main board's sheet 720 heater LED drive rows; the pre-order check's report for the wall design (50 known layout-time and owner-read lines, not judged here); drawings of the five sheets from `task hw:sheets` with raster views.
- **Datasheets opened this round** (curl and pdftotext, or read as images where the sheet is a drawing): Lite-On MOC3083 series DS70-2001-027 Rev B, effective 04/28/2010, footer BNS-OD-C131/A4, 8 numbered pages (the LCSC-hosted copy named by parts-specification.yaml; cover page and all pages read, pins and curves seen as images); STMicroelectronics BTA16/BTB16/T1610/T1635 Doc ID 7471 Rev 9, March 2010 (web.archive.org copy of the st.com CD00002265 address), and DS2114 Rev 11, May 2018 (the current st.com bta16.pdf), compared figure by figure for the figures the records use (IGT, VGT, VGD, IH, IL, (dV/dt)c, Rth(j-c), Tj, ITSM, VDSM, 2500 VRMS) and found the same; Zhuhai Hongjiacheng 07D series Rev 1.2, 6 pages (LCSC datasheet for C49072860); UNI-ROYAL Thick Film Chip Resistors Data Sheet V.3, 12 February 2019 (LCSC datasheet for C18002); XFCN PZ254V-11 drawing REV A1 (LCSC datasheet for C2937625); TE Connectivity drawing C-63849 rev F1 (read as an image).
- **Reviewer**: one reviewer, a Claude Sonnet 5.5 session, working from the commit, the netlist, the drawings, the records and datasheets above; the fanned-out form was not used.

### Verdicts

| PINV-1 | /+5V_WB | pass | netlist pins J301.1, U101.1, U201.1; Lite-On MOC3083 series DS70-2001-027 Rev B (effective 04/28/2010; footer BNS-OD-C131/A4) p.5 (IF 50 mA, VR 6 V), p.6 (VF 1.4 V max at 20 mA); pin-limits.yaml domain supply5_wb 5.25 V; main-board extract sheet 720 (R763/R773 180 ohm, +5V_WB 4.75 to 5.25 V) | Anode net is a supply from the main board, 5.25 V at most, never below 0 V; the LED current is limited by R763/R773 on the main board (circuit-proof.md row a: 29.5 mA bound against 50 mA); J301 is a passive header. |

| PARTS-1 | U101 | pass | Lite-On MOC3083 series DS70-2001-027 Rev B (effective 04/28/2010; footer BNS-OD-C131/A4) p.2 outline (pins), p.5 to p.6 (IF 50 mA max; note 3 p.6: recommended IF between max IFT 5 mA and 50 mA); circuit-proof.md rows a and b; main-board extract sheet 720 (R763/R773) | The sheet lists no required external part and gives no application circuit. The one external part its notes call for, a current limit setting IF between 5 mA and 50 mA, is the 180 ohm R763/R773 on the main board (IF 17.9 to 29.5 mA); the gate network R101/R102 (R201/R202) is the maker-independent surge limit of row b. Pin 5 (substrate) and pin 3 are not connected, as the pin table asks. |

| CAPD-1 | none | not applicable | netlist: no capacitor | The schematic carries no ceramic capacitor. |
| BUSL-1 | /HEAT_C_LED_K | pass | netlist pins J301.2, U101.2; main-board extract sheet 720 and its heater LED drive rows (AO3400A sink, 180 ohm R763/R773 from +5V_WB); Lite-On MOC3083 series DS70-2001-027 Rev B (effective 04/28/2010; footer BNS-OD-C131/A4) p.6 (VF, IFT) | The net is the open-drain return of an LED load, not a signal bus: the load that takes the pull-up's place is the LED and the 180 ohm resistor on the main board, which limits the current to 17.9 to 29.5 mA (smallest-resistance limit met by a wide margin against the sink's 5.7 A rating), and the line is a static drive with no speed mode or rise-time limit (100 Hz firing, cable capacitance times 180 ohm is nanoseconds). One pull-up-equivalent to the rail that defines the level, +5V_WB. |
| BUSL-1 | /HEAT_S_LED_K | pass | netlist pins J301.3, U201.2; main-board extract sheet 720 and its heater LED drive rows (AO3400A sink, 180 ohm R763/R773 from +5V_WB); Lite-On MOC3083 series DS70-2001-027 Rev B (effective 04/28/2010; footer BNS-OD-C131/A4) p.6 (VF, IFT) | The net is the open-drain return of an LED load, not a signal bus: the load that takes the pull-up's place is the LED and the 180 ohm resistor on the main board, which limits the current to 17.9 to 29.5 mA (smallest-resistance limit met by a wide margin against the sink's 5.7 A rating), and the line is a static drive with no speed mode or rise-time limit (100 Hz firing, cable capacitance times 180 ohm is nanoseconds). One pull-up-equivalent to the rail that defines the level, +5V_WB. |
| STRAP-1 | none | not applicable | netlist: no integrated circuit with boot, strap or mode pins | The opto-triacs have no pin sampled at reset or power-up and no input the sheet says must not float; their only unused pins are 3 (N.C.) and 5 (substrate), flagged no-connect as the pin table asks. |
| STRAP-2 | none | not applicable | pin-allocation.yaml controller: none; netlist | The schematic carries no controller. |
| RCFL-1 | none | not applicable | netlist: no resistor-capacitor pair and no converter input | No converter input and no capacitor. |
| SUPPLY-1 | /+5V_WB | pass | netlist: no capacitor on the net; XFCN PZ254V-11 drawing REV A1 p.1 (3 A); Lite-On MOC3083 series DS70-2001-027 Rev B (effective 04/28/2010; footer BNS-OD-C131/A4) p.5 (IF 50 mA) | The board holds no capacitance on the rail: the uncharged load is two LEDs, so the first-applied current is the steady 29.5 mA at most per LED (circuit-proof.md row a), 59 mA together, against J301's 3 A and the main board's limiting resistor. |
| SUPPLY-1 | J301 | pass | XFCN PZ254V-11 drawing REV A1 p.1 (current rating 3 A, -40 to +105 degC); circuit-proof.md row a | The header passes 59 mA at most; no inrush figure beyond it arises because nothing on this board stores charge. |
| SUPPLY-2 | none | not applicable | Lite-On MOC3083 series DS70-2001-027 Rev B (effective 04/28/2010; footer BNS-OD-C131/A4) p.5 to p.6 (no minimum supply voltage stated); netlist | The rail powers LEDs only. The sheet gives no minimum operating voltage below which an output is undefined: with the rail low the LED current falls below IFT and the output does not fire (fails off), and the output stage is zero-cross inhibited above VINH 20 V max. No controller, drive stage or front end sits on the rail on this board. |
| POLAR-1 | U101 | pass | Lite-On MOC3083 series DS70-2001-027 Rev B (effective 04/28/2010; footer BNS-OD-C131/A4) p.2 outline (pin 1 anode, pin 2 cathode), p.5 (VR 6 V); netlist pins 1 on /+5V_WB and 2 on the LED_K net; sheet drawing Coffee/Steam heater switch | The symbol's pin 1 is the anode and pin 2 the cathode as the pin table has them; the anode net is the supply, the cathode net the sink, so the LED is forward-biased when driven; the reverse voltage across it can be at most the 5.25 V rail, under VR 6 V, in any state the main board's rows list. |
| POLAR-1 | U201 | pass | Lite-On MOC3083 series DS70-2001-027 Rev B (effective 04/28/2010; footer BNS-OD-C131/A4) p.2 outline (pin 1 anode, pin 2 cathode), p.5 (VR 6 V); netlist pins 1 on /+5V_WB and 2 on the LED_K net; sheet drawing Coffee/Steam heater switch | The symbol's pin 1 is the anode and pin 2 the cathode as the pin table has them; the anode net is the supply, the cathode net the sink, so the LED is forward-biased when driven; the reverse voltage across it can be at most the 5.25 V rail, under VR 6 V, in any state the main board's rows list. |
| POLAR-1 | T101 | pass | ST BTA16/BTB16/T1610/T1635 Doc ID 7471 Rev 9 (March 2010) p.1 package pictures (A1, A2, G left to right from the front); netlist pins 1 on /RET, 2 on OUT_x, 3 on the gate network; ST BTA16/BTB16/T1610/T1635 Doc ID 7471 Rev 9 (March 2010) Table 4 p.3 (IGT quadrants) | The triac's terminals are distinguished: pin 1 is A1 (MT1) on RET, pin 2 A2 on the element lead, pin 3 the gate, matching the sheet's order; the gate is driven from A2 through the opto-triac so it is referenced to A1 as the sheet's quadrant definitions need (quadrants I and III). |
| POLAR-1 | T201 | pass | ST BTA16/BTB16/T1610/T1635 Doc ID 7471 Rev 9 (March 2010) p.1 package pictures (A1, A2, G left to right from the front); netlist pins 1 on /RET, 2 on OUT_x, 3 on the gate network; ST BTA16/BTB16/T1610/T1635 Doc ID 7471 Rev 9 (March 2010) Table 4 p.3 (IGT quadrants) | The triac's terminals are distinguished: pin 1 is A1 (MT1) on RET, pin 2 A2 on the element lead, pin 3 the gate, matching the sheet's order; the gate is driven from A2 through the opto-triac so it is referenced to A1 as the sheet's quadrant definitions need (quadrants I and III). |
| CABLE-1 | none | not applicable | netlist; division record passage on the return landing and joiners (added-documents/division.txt); pin-allocation.yaml | The board's connectors do not mate a retained or bought harness. J301 takes a new three-conductor cable made for this machine (pin-allocation.yaml names each position); J101, J201 and J401 each take a new wire with a female push-on made for this machine: the retained element leads end on in-line joiners, not on these blades. The connectors J101, J201, J401 have one contact each, so no pin order or mirroring arises. Which retained element lead is the coffee or the steam one rests on the owner's trace in connector-pinouts.md, outside this board. |

### Findings

| Finding | Raised by | Severity | What and where | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | PINV-1 | function | Nets /Coffee heater switch/OUT_C, /Steam heater switch/OUT_S and /RET (T101 and T201 pins 1 and 2, U101 and U201 pin 4, RV101 and RV201, J101, J201, J401) can see a surge clamped by the varistor at 775 V (07D471K, 10 A 8/20 us, Zhuhai Hongjiacheng Rev 1.2 p.1), above the triac's VDRM/VRRM 600 V and VDSM 700 V for 10 ms (ST 7471 Rev 9 Tables 1 and 2); the nets /Coffee heater switch/HEAT_C_OPTO_MT and HEAT_C_GATE_R (and the steam pair) carry 775 V / 396 ohm = 1.96 A through U101 pin 6 or U201 pin 6, against ITSM 1 A at 100 us (Lite-On p.5). Neither sheet states a microsecond withstand, so nothing on the schematic holds the nets inside the pins' ratings. circuit-proof.md row d records the gap, and the owner holds D7 (varistors kept); the item still fails until a part or a sourced rating closes it. | fixed: commit 8463c446 (triac BTA16-800BWRG, opto-triac onsemi MOC3083M; the seven PINV-1 rows it failed pass in round 2); owner-sign-off.md W-1 and W-3 (accepted_by: David Baker; accepted_on: 2026-11-18) |
| 1.2 | FAULT-1 | safety | The chain from the LED return to the triac (U101, R101, R102, T101, RV101 coffee; U201, R201, R202, T201, RV201 steam) has no list of failure modes with an outcome for each: circuit-proof.md holds only a gate-to-MT1 short (row b2) and the held-off leakage (row c). Not listed: triac failed short, opto-triac output failed short, a varistor failed short (each leaves the heater on), a gate resistor open or short, the opto-triac LED return shorted to ground on the cable (the record says it fires the heater, 'reported as an open item'). Where an outcome is named it rests on the main board's interlock relay K201 and its supply-current check, which act on the controller's command or firmware, not on the chain's own means as the item requires, and the record names no test interval or hold-off until a person attends. | overridden by: REQ-SAFETY-CHAIN-001.C8 (each of these outcomes leaves a heater on with its hazard bounded by the element's own series thermostats and cutoffs, an element independent of the failed part and of the controller; DEC-CHAIN-FAULT-BOUNDED-BY-INDEPENDENT-ELEMENT addresses that criterion); accepted_by: David Baker (owner-sign-off.md W-6, in his words "Accept 1 2 and 3"); accepted_on: 2026-11-19. The partly failed varistor outcome is not closed here: it is carried as 2.4 to REQ-ACTUATION-CONFIRMATION-001.C10 (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps"); fixed: circuit-proof.md failure modes of the heater chain |
| 1.3 | BARRIER-1 | safety | U101 and U201 (Lite-On MOC3083) form the heater-switch barrier, declared reinforced in separation-basis.yaml (10.0 mm creepage, 3.0 mm clearance, 4.0 kV impulse). The sheet gives Viso 5000 Vrms for 1 minute only (p.5): no creepage, clearance, impulse or working-isolation voltage, no reinforced certification (VDE only with the 'V' ordering option, p.1), and the DIP-6 pin row spacing is 7.62 mm, under the 10.0 mm creepage. Every rating but the withstand is absent, so the part is not shown to meet the declared barrier. The owner holds the acceptance (constraints-figures.md); it is not recorded as accepted_by in this review. | carried: the owner's recorded decision to carry it to a named later step (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps") to the owner's re-declaration of separation-basis.yaml before U911 and U921 are ordered, which SOL-HW-HEATER-WALL-BOARD-PROVEN-AND-LAID-OUT.C5 and SOL-HW-SEPARATION-BASIS-SIGNED.C2 ask for; accepted_by: David Baker (owner-sign-off.md W-2 and W-8, his word "Yes" to the carry); accepted_on: 2026-11-18 (W-2) and 2026-11-19 (W-8) |
| 1.4 | BARRIER-1 | safety | T101 and T201's insulated tab to the machine's metal wall is the basic barrier to earth that the heater-switch entry of separation-basis.yaml declares. The ST sheet states 2500 VRMS only (Rev 9 p.1; DS2114 Rev 11 p.1), with no creepage, clearance or impulse figure for the package, against the basis's 1.5 mm, 2.6 mm and 2.5 kV basic rows; the bond to the wall is unmeasured and D15 holds the unlisted wall-to-copper pair. | carried: the owner's recorded decision to carry it to a named later step (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps") to the owner's re-declaration of separation-basis.yaml before the parts are ordered (SOL-HW-HEATER-WALL-BOARD-PROVEN-AND-LAID-OUT.C5, SOL-HW-SEPARATION-BASIS-SIGNED.C2) and to the earth-bond measurement at bring-up (DEL-HW-BRING-UP.C6); accepted_by: David Baker (owner-sign-off.md W-4 and W-8, his word "Yes" to the carry); accepted_on: 2026-11-18 (W-4) and 2026-11-19 (W-8) |
| 1.5 | SURGE-1 | safety | The mains-referenced nets of both heater branches are protected by RV101 and RV201 only, and no record gives the surge they see with its source impedance (the 2.5 kV of overvoltage category II is in the basis; the generator impedance is not sourced, open-items.md), the varistor's rating at that waveform (the sheet gives 1200 A once and 29 J at 8/20 us and 10/1000 us, not 1.2/50 us), or a clamp at or below what sits behind it: 775 V at 10 A exceeds the triac's 600 V and is within 25 V of the opto-triac's 800 V. No overcurrent device in series with the varistor is on this board; the main-board entry fuse is the only one and no record shows it clears a failed-short varistor on this branch. | carried: the owner's recorded decision to carry it to a named later step (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps") to REQ-ELECTRICAL-INSTALL-001.C10 (transients leave the separation intact and the protective chain working, from makers' ratings; the surge inputs stay unsourced), and REQ-ACTUATION-CONFIRMATION-001.C10 (the 2.0 A detection); DEL-HW-HEATER-WALL-BOARD-DESIGNED.C19 is not asked of the carry (it asks the surge rating relied on once the clamps are dropped; the clamps are kept); accepted_by: David Baker (owner-sign-off.md W-3 and W-8, his word "Yes" to the carry); accepted_on: 2026-11-18 (W-3) and 2026-11-19 (W-8); fixed: circuit-proof.md row (d) surge table |
| 1.6 | ESD-1 | function | J301 (three-way header to the main board) and the blades J101, J201 and J401 are conductors a person can reach in service and no record declares a discharge level or shows a clamp or a barrier behind each (the main board's review records the same gap as its 1.18). J301's lines go to the LED pins behind the barrier part, with no clamp or series element on this board; the blades land on the 600 V triac pins. | carried: the owner's recorded decision to carry it to a named later step (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps") to REQ-ELECTRICAL-INSTALL-001.C11 (protection at the declared discharge level); accepted_by: Claude, on behalf of David Baker, under his standing instruction of 2026-11-07 (owner-sign-off.md W-5b, not in his words); accepted_on: 2026-11-18; continued as 2.5 |
| 1.7 | DERATE-1 | quality | derating-policy.yaml gives the blade terminal and joiner class shares of 1 for voltage, current and temperature with sources that state none ('no voltage figure is held', 'unmet by any source', '15 A carried and unsourced'), and J301's header class is not in the policy (parts-specification.yaml gives it class none); a factor with no source and a class omitted. | fixed: derating-policy.yaml (header class sourced); the blade and joiner class carried: the owner's recorded decision to carry it to a named later step (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps") to DEL-HW-HEATER-WALL-BOARD-DESIGNED.C20 (rating recorded with its source, unsourced counting as absent at the carried 15 A); accepted_by: Claude, on behalf of David Baker, under his standing instruction of 2026-11-07 (owner-sign-off.md W-5a, not in his words); accepted_on: 2026-11-18; continued as 2.6 |
| 1.8 | DERATE-2 | quality | T101 and T201 are not within their derated rating: at the provisional 80.0 x 30.0 mm window the steady junction is 116.4 degC (best case, 45 degC air) against the policy's 100 degC, and 141.4 degC or 168.8 degC at 70 degC against 125 degC (circuit-proof.md row e and grading table; controller-board-division.md D11). The record states it; the rating is not stretched. Also unread: the sheet's IT(RMS) against case temperature curve (Rev 9 Figure 2), which would give the current margin at the case temperature (114.8 to 116.1 degC) the judgement implies. | carried: the owner's recorded decision to carry it to a named later step (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps") to DEL-HW-BRING-UP.C11 (case temperature at sustained full heat) and DEL-HW-HEATER-WALL-BOARD-DESIGNED.C22 (the wall's grade and thickness at the cut); accepted_by: David Baker (owner-sign-off.md W-5c, in his words "accept 2.7 with the bring-up case-temperature measurement and the cut-time checks of the wall's grade and thickness"); accepted_on: 2026-11-19; continued as 2.7 |
| 1.9 | DERATE-2 | quality | U101 and U201: the worst-case part temperature is not derived. The sheet gives TA 100 degC and no thermal resistance or junction limit; the policy's 0.88 share (88 degC of a 100 degC ambient limit) is not applied, and circuit-proof.md says the temperature beside a triac case at 100 degC or more (20.0 mm away) 'is not computed and not measured'. Fig. 2 (ITM 0.1 A to 40 degC falling to 0.02 A at 100 degC) is not used against the 50 mA gate pulse. The 70 degC air figure plus an unknown neighbour rise cannot be shown under the share. | carried: the owner's recorded decision to carry it to a named later step (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps") to DEL-HW-BRING-UP.C11 (the opto-triac cases at sustained full heat) and DEL-HW-BRING-UP.C13 (the air figure the parts were graded to), with layout hedge 12 as the remedy; accepted_by: Claude, on behalf of David Baker, under his standing instruction of 2026-11-07 (owner-sign-off.md W-5d, not in his words); accepted_on: 2026-11-19; continued as 2.8 |

## Round 2

- **Round**: 2
- **Round kind**: full (every item of the circuit practice catalogue against every net, part and sheet; round 1 judged the schematic before the design change of 2026-11-18, this round judges the redrawn schematic).
- **Schematic judged**: git commit 8463c446 (hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board/heater-wall-board.kicad_sch and its four sheets 100, 200, 300, 400; worktree clean at that commit).
- **Netlist**: `/Applications/KiCad/KiCad.app/Contents/MacOS/kicad-cli sch export netlist --output /tmp/wall-review2/review.net hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board/heater-wall-board.kicad_sch` (run from the repository root; KiCad 10.0.7; 14 parts, 16 nets of which 4 are the `unconnected-` no-connect nets of U911 and U921, 5 sheets including the root).
- **Records read**: hardware/sunbeam-em7000/heater-wall-board/ parts-specification.yaml, derating-policy.yaml, pin-limits.yaml, pin-allocation.yaml, catalogue-extract.yaml, board-rules.yaml, manufacturer-limits.yaml, circuit-proof.md with circuit-proof/W_margins.py and W_margins.log (re-run: exit 0, 16 PASS, output identical to the log), circuit-verdicts.md, bring-up-provision.md, open-items.md, layout-hedges.md, fabrication-notes.md, constraints-figures.md, schematic-spec.yaml, schematic-review.md round 1; hardware/sunbeam-em7000/separation-basis.yaml (accepted_on 2026-11-18; the heater-switch barrier's formed_by names no part rating and no exception, the entries name no reference of the 9xx series, and no reference of the 9xx series appears in main-board/ or the basis); docs/sunbeam-em7000/controller-board-design-constraints.md and controller-board-division.md (The heater wall board; The heater wall assembly, judged) as extracted for round 1 (/tmp/wall-review/review/added-documents) and re-read where cited; the main board's sheet 720 extract; .stage0/wall-design-opinion.md and .stage0/wall-change-round-report.md for why the parts changed (the circuit is judged on the datasheets, not on their say-so).
- **Pre-order check**: `python3 /tmp/wl/wallonly.py <clone at 8463c446>` (the wall design only, in a `git clone --no-hardlinks` of the worktree): exit 1, 50 lines for the wall design, all layout-time, project-file or catalogue lines: 14 missing_footprint, 9 ignored_check, 8 workmanship_figure, 8 board_minimums, 6 parts_catalogue (C10065, C23552, C49072860 not in the catalogue extract), 2 workmanship_rule, 1 mains_declaration (text variable MAINS unset), 1 invalid_outline, 1 custom_rules; the other six designs PASS. No schematic-side line (parts_part, derated_voltage, pin_limit, connectivity, board_rules_record, manufacturer_limits_record) is reported, so the drawn fields, the derated voltages and the pin limits agree with the records. Not judged here.
- **Datasheets opened this round** (each opened at its address on 2026-11-18, curl and pdftotext, plots and drawings also read as images): onsemi MOC3081M, MOC3082M, MOC3083M 6-Pin DIP Zero-Cross Triac Driver Optocoupler (800 V Peak), publication order number MOC3083M/D, Rev. 2, September 2022, 13 pages (https://www.onsemi.com/pdf/datasheet/moc3083m-d.pdf; the address the schematic's Datasheet property carries, https://www.onsemi.com/pub/Collateral/MOC3083M-D.PDF, returns a byte-identical file) [ON]; STMicroelectronics T1610, T1635, T1650, BTA16, BTB16 Snubberless, logic level and standard 16 A Triacs, DS2114 Rev 11, May 2018, 18 pages (the Internet Archive's copy of https://www.st.com/resource/en/datasheet/bta16.pdf; st.com itself returned nothing to curl) [ST]; UNI-ROYAL Thick Film Chip Resistors Data Sheet V.3, 12 Feb 2019, 9 pages (the LCSC link of C18002) [UR]; Zhuhai Hongjiacheng (R+O) 07D series varistors, Rev:1.2, 6 pages (LCSC link of C49072860) [ZH]; XFCN PZ254V-11 drawing REV A1, 1 page (LCSC link of C2937625) [XF]; TE Connectivity drawing C-63849 rev F1, 1 page (LCSC link of C86469, read as an image) [TE]. The LCSC links of C23552 and C10065 serve older sheets, a Fairchild MOC3081M/2M/3M of September 2010 (VISO 7500 Vac(pk) for 1 s) and an ST BTA16 REV. 7 of February 2006 (9 pages); they were read for comparison and no verdict rests on them. LCSC product pages were read for part identity only (C10065 is BTA16-800BWRG, C23552 is MOC3083M, C18002 is 1206W4F2000T5E, C49072860 is 07D471K, C2937625 is PZ254V-11-03P, C86469 is 63849-1); stock and price were not read.
- **Citation key**: ON, ST, UR, ZH, XF and TE as above; "p." is the document's own page; circuit-proof.md rows are cited as the record labels them.
- **Reviewer**: one reviewer, a Claude Sonnet 5.5 session that did not draw the schematic and did not judge round 1, working from the commit, the netlist, the drawings (all five sheets rendered and looked at), the records and the datasheets above; the fanned-out form was not used.
- **Owner decisions of 2026-11-18, as the caller of this round states them** (not yet entered in any file at this commit, so none of them closes a finding below until it is): the 800 V triac (BTA16-800BWRG); the onsemi MOC3083M with its 7 mm creepage exception; the 07D471K varistor kept with the residual risk and the firmware stuck-on threshold of 2.0 A or less, to be carried into the graph; the D15 entries (the 1206 gate-resistor pad pairs, the TO-220 pin pairs, the varistor leads, the 2.6 mm wall-to-copper gap, the triac tab to the earthed bracket on ST's UL 1557 2500 Vrms). Known and not raised: the blank title blocks (as on the main board's sheets), the mirrored opto-triac drawing, the layout-time hedges of layout-hedges.md (judged below only for whether the schematic and records carry them correctly: hedges 4 and 5 and the D15 pairs correspond to the basis entries still to be made, finding 2.3; hedge 7 is the varistor risk of 2.4; hedge 10 is bring-up C13, 2.3 and 2.8), and the stock and price of C10065, C23552 and C49072860.

### Findings

| Finding | Raised by | Severity | What and where | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | FAULT-1 | safety | Chain U911, R911, R912, T911, RV911 (coffee) and U921, R921, R922, T921, RV921 (steam). The failure-mode table now in circuit-proof.md lists every part and mode (this half of round 1's 1.2 is met), but every outcome that leaves the heater on (triac short, opto-triac output short, varistor short, LED return shorted to ground on the cable, a partly failed varistor at up to 340 W) is caught by the main board's interlock relay K201 and its supply-current check, by the firmware's stuck-on current threshold, or by the element's own cutoffs, none of which is the chain's own means as the item requires; the table gives no test interval and no hold-off until a person attends ("the interlock's test interval and hold-off are the main board's records and are not restated or sourced here"). The 2.0 A or less threshold the varistor risk needs is accepted by the owner on 2026-11-18 but is not yet a node in the graph and no firmware parameter carries it (open-items.md D7). | overridden by: REQ-SAFETY-CHAIN-001.C8 (each of these outcomes leaves a heater on with its hazard bounded by the element's own series thermostats and cutoffs, an element independent of the failed part and of the controller; DEC-CHAIN-FAULT-BOUNDED-BY-INDEPENDENT-ELEMENT addresses that criterion); accepted_by: David Baker (owner-sign-off.md W-6, in his words "Accept 1 2 and 3"); accepted_on: 2026-11-19. The partly failed varistor outcome is not closed here: it is carried as 2.4 to REQ-ACTUATION-CONFIRMATION-001.C10 (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps"); fixed: circuit-proof.md failure-mode table and INDEP-1 section |
| 2.2 | BARRIER-1 | safety | U911 and U921 (onsemi MOC3083M) form the heater-switch barrier, declared reinforced in separation-basis.yaml (10.0 mm creepage, 3.0 mm clearance, 4.0 kV impulse, material group IIIa, pollution degree 2). ON Rev. 2 p.2 gives creepage and clearance at least 7 mm, VIORM 850 Vpk (against 357.8 V peak), VIOTM 6000 Vpk (against 4.0 kV), CTI 175 and p.4 VISO 4170 Vrms for 1 minute: clearance, impulse and working voltage are met and creepage is 3.0 mm under the declared 10.0 mm (above the 6.4 mm reinforced floor of the 320 V row). The sheet never calls the part reinforced, its insulation table is headed "as per DIN EN/IEC 60747-5-5" while p.9 gives that standard's option only to the V parts (MOC3083VM) and C23552 is the non-V MOC3083M; the record accepts the sheet for it (constraints-figures.md). Owner-decided: on 2026-11-18 the owner accepted the part and the 7 mm creepage exception. The acceptance is entered nowhere yet: separation-basis.yaml's heater-switch formed_by names no part, rating or exception (the pump-drive entry names U701 and U711 and signs theirs), and this record carries no accepted_by and accepted_on, which step 5 requires of a safety finding closed by a reason. | carried: the owner's recorded decision to carry it to a named later step (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps") to the owner's re-declaration of separation-basis.yaml before U911 and U921 are ordered (open-items.md "What the owner must re-declare in the separation basis before the barrier parts are ordered"), which SOL-HW-HEATER-WALL-BOARD-PROVEN-AND-LAID-OUT.C5 and SOL-HW-SEPARATION-BASIS-SIGNED.C2 ask for; accepted_by: David Baker (owner-sign-off.md W-2 and W-4 and W-8, his word "Yes" to the carry); accepted_on: 2026-11-18 (W-2 and W-4) and 2026-11-19 (W-8) |
| 2.3 | BARRIER-1 | safety | T911 and T921's insulated tab to the machine's metal wall is the basic barrier to earth of the heater-switch entry. ST Rev 11 p.1 gives 2500 VRMS and UL1557 file E81734 only: no creepage, clearance or impulse figure for the package, against the basis's 1.5 mm, 2.6 mm and 2.5 kV basic rows; the bond to the wall is unmeasured (bring-up C13). The D15 pairs the layout will make (R911, R912, R921, R922 pad pairs, the TO-220 pin pairs, the varistor leads, the 2.6 mm wall-to-copper gap) have no entry in separation-basis.yaml, whose entries name the main board's references and nets only. | TOUCH-1 | none | not applicable | netlist | No filter or supply capacitor connects a mains conductor to protective earth or to an accessible conductive part: the only parts on mains nets are the opto-triac output, triac, varistor and gate resistors, all between mains conductors. |
| INDEP-1 | none | not applicable | netlist; circuit-proof.md 'Independence of the heater chain from the controller'; main-board circuit-verdicts.md (Whole board and Interlock rows) | The board holds no watchdog-held switch, timer or latch: the element that removes power on loss of proof of life is the main board's interlock relay K201, not on this board. Traced for the three things the item names: clock and oscillator (none on this board, no net reaches one), reset (no reset net, no net to a reset output), supply (the only supply is +5V_WB, an LED supply from the main board's +5V that powers no logic here; losing it unlights both LEDs and holds both triacs off, the direction that removes power, row (c)). The command to turn on is the controller's; the failure of a part of this chain to turn off is FAULT-1, not this item. |

### Findings

| Finding | Raised by | Severity | What and where | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1 | FAULT-1 | safety | Chain U911, R911, R912, T911, RV911 (coffee) and U921, R921, R922, T921, RV921 (steam); continues 1.2 and 2.1. The failure-mode table now gives every part and mode and, since this round's records, how and when each is detected, honestly: no interval or hold-off is stated where a record states none, the power-up leakage check is delivered by no node, and the firmware stuck-on threshold has no node. What is missing is the acceptance. Every outcome that leaves the heater on (triac short, opto-triac output short, varistor short, LED return shorted on the cable, a partly failed varistor) rests on the element's own thermostats and cutoffs, K201 (which opens only if the controller stops refreshing) and a firmware threshold. owner-sign-off.md names finding 2.1 and 1.2 in no entry: W-2 closes 1.3 and 2.2, W-3 the varistor findings 1.1, 1.5 and 2.4, W-4 1.4 and 2.3 and the barrier half of 2.2, W-5a, W-5b and W-5c findings 2.5, 2.6, 2.7 and 2.9, and the preamble's general words about K201, the fuse and the threshold name no finding. The record cites no decision for the third outcome the item admits (DEC-CHAIN-FAULT-BOUNDED-BY-INDEPENDENT-ELEMENT: an independent element still bounds the hazard) and names no source here for what the thermostats are (the main board's own decision text says the reference machine does not establish whether they reset). The 2.0 A or less threshold that W-3 and the table rely on is in no graph node at 714ca855 (search of the graph; open-items.md says the main session authors it). A safety finding closes only by a change or a named person's acceptance of a reason. Failure-mode table and INDEP-1 statement of circuit-proof.md; owner-sign-off.md. | overridden by: REQ-SAFETY-CHAIN-001.C8 (each of these outcomes leaves a heater on with its hazard bounded by the element's own series thermostats and cutoffs, an element independent of the failed part and of the controller; DEC-CHAIN-FAULT-BOUNDED-BY-INDEPENDENT-ELEMENT addresses that criterion); accepted_by: David Baker (owner-sign-off.md W-6, in his words "Accept 1 2 and 3"); accepted_on: 2026-11-19. The partly failed varistor outcome is not closed here: it is carried as 2.4 to REQ-ACTUATION-CONFIRMATION-001.C10 (owner-sign-off.md "Carried to named later steps"); fixed: circuit-proof.md failure modes and INDEP-1 section, owner-sign-off.md W-6, open-items.md requirement paragraph |

### Round 2 findings, closure

Each of round 2's findings read against the records and the schematic at 714ca855, and against the datasheets re-opened this round. The method has the designer write Closed by; the reviewer has written none, and this table says what Closed by can validly carry. 'Closed' means the cause is gone or a named person's acceptance exists for a safety finding, with the arithmetic it relies on checked. A safety finding's Closed by must carry the person's name and date after the reason (`accepted_by: David Baker, accepted_on: 2026-11-18`, entries W-2, W-3, W-4 of owner-sign-off.md, which quote his words).

| Round 2 finding | Closure verdict at 714ca855 |
|---|---|
| 2.1 FAULT-1 (outcomes rest on K201, firmware, element cutoffs; no interval or hold-off) | Open, continued as 3.1. Half closed: the table and the four detecting means, with interval and hold-off stated where a record states one and 'not stated' where none does, answer the 'no test interval or hold-off' complaint honestly (the leakage check once per power-up and delivered by no node; K201 opens within 100 ms only if the controller stops refreshing). What is not closed is the acceptance: no owner-sign-off entry names 1.2 or 2.1, and the 2.0 A threshold has no graph node. |
| 2.2 BARRIER-1 (opto-triac, creepage under the declared 10.0 mm; acceptance entered nowhere) | Closed by W-2 (and W-4 for the board spacing), David Baker in his words, 2026-11-18. Arithmetic checked against ON Rev. 2: VIORM 850 Vpk against 357.8 V peak, VIOTM 6000 Vpk against the 4.0 kV impulse, CTI 175 (material group IIIa, as the basis), p.2; VISO 4170 Vrms, p.4; creepage and clearance at least 7 mm, over the 6.4 mm reinforced floor of the 320 V row the basis derives and under the declared 10.0 mm; the p.9 statement that the 60747-5-5 option is on the V parts only is recorded in W-2. Suggested Closed by: `met:` the reinforced floor is met at 7 mm and the 10.0 mm declaration is the owner's accepted exception, `accepted_by: David Baker`, `accepted_on: 2026-11-18`. Ordering gate, not a schematic item: the owner re-declares separation-basis.yaml before the parts are ordered (open-items.md lists the four entries; the basis still names no part of this board). |
| 2.3 BARRIER-1 (triac tab to wall; D15 pairs not in the basis) | Closed by W-4, David Baker in his words, 2026-11-18: the tab on ST's UL 1557 2500 Vrms (ST p.1; no creepage, clearance or impulse figure exists to cite, recorded as such), the pad pairs and the 2.6 mm gap, with the earth bond measured at bring-up (DEL-HW-BRING-UP.C13). Same basis re-declaration before ordering as 2.2. |
| 2.4 SURGE-1 (clamp inputs unsourced; dv/dt; 11.7 A case; partly failed varistor) | Closed by W-3, David Baker in his words, 2026-11-18, with one dependency raised in 3.1. Arithmetic checked: ZH p.2 775 V at 10 A, 1200 A once, 29 J; 800 V on both parts leaves 25 V (3.1 %); 253 V squared over (4 x 47 ohm) = 340 W and 2.69 A rms (W_margins.py re-run identical); (1200 V - 750 V) / 47 ohm = 9.6 A, so the 'holds for any entry clamp up to about 1.2 kV' statement is consistent; 775 V / 1.2 us = 646 V/us against ON p.4 static dv/dt 600 V/us minimum and 1500 typical (the surge exceeds the guaranteed minimum by 8 %, and the record says a false trigger is not excluded and what follows, one half cycle of nuisance conduction, 13.6 J, with the persistence requirement). The unsourced inputs (2 ohm source impedance, 1 kV level, entry clamp above 50 A, the 07D471K's clamp away from 10 A, the opto-triac's turn-on time) are each marked unsourced in the record and the sheet has no V-I curve to source the last. The acceptance relies on the firmware threshold of 2.0 A or less, which is a requirement and not yet a graph node. |
| 2.5 ESD-1 (no level, no clamp) | Carried as an owner-held residual, W-5b (on the owner's behalf; function severity, no person's name is required). Honest record: no level declared, no clamp, handled with ESD care at fitting, what would return it stated. Not re-raised. Step 5 has no 'carried' form (the main board needed DEC-SCHEMATIC-REVIEW-FINDINGS-CARRIED-BY-OWNER, which closed its own ESD finding 5.18 to a layout-review step), so the designer chooses the Closed by wording. |
| 2.6 DERATE-1 (blade and joiner factors unsourced) | Carried, W-5a: the header class is sourced from XF p.1; the blade and joiner factors remain 'carried 15 A, unsourced', recorded as such, with the act that would source it (TE application specification 114-2115, named on TE C-63849 rev F1, not obtained). Not re-raised. |
| 2.7 DERATE-2 (triacs over their derated share; Figure 3 read) | The record now reads ST Figure 3 (p.4) and states the negative current margin: 3.85 A (chord) to 4.51 A (model) against the steam element's 5.38 A at the best-case case temperature of 106.2 degC; chord rechecked, 16 A x (125 - 106.2) / 39 = 7.7 A, half 3.85 A; the plot is concave so the model's 9.0 A is the better read. W-5c states 'closes nothing' and carries it to the owner's wall-cut revisit (D11) and the bring-up case temperature. Carried honestly; not re-raised. |
| 2.8 DERATE-2 (opto-triac neighbour rise unmeasured) | Carried to bring-up (DEL-HW-BRING-UP.C13, a row of bring-up-provision.md) and hedge 12; the record says the 70 degC case is not shown and the 340 K/W and 10.7 K are inferences (ON p.3 gives 2.94 mW/degC total-power derating and no thermal resistance; 1/0.00294 = 340 K/W). Not re-raised. |
| 2.9 DERATE-2 (blades unrated) | Carried, W-5a. TE C-63849 rev F1 re-read: no current, voltage or temperature rating; names application specification 114-2115. Bring-up thermocouple step bounds it on the machine. Not re-raised. |
| 2.10 free pass (stale fields and records) | Closed in substance: the instance Description of T911 and T921 and the Datasheet of U911 and U921 are corrected; bring-up-provision.md now states VF 1.5 V max and 17.87 mA; the leakage figure, the IDRM2 and stock sentences, the unconnected-net name and the UNI-ROYAL 'not opened' text are corrected. Two residues remain and are raised: the library cache and the netlist's libsource still carry 600 V and the old address (3.2), and page citations to ZH and UR do not match the documents (3.3). |
| 2.11 free pass (hold-off stated at 25 degC; hot corner) | Closed. Row (c) now states the hold-off at 25 degC where the sheet gives IDRM1's maximum, scales it by two readings of the typical curve for 85 and 100 degC (x75, x250) and by ST Figure 9 (IGT x0.45 at 125 degC, confirmed on p.5), gives x30 and x13 at 85 degC and x9 and x4 at 100 degC, and says in terms that the margin is an estimate from typical curves and that the bring-up leakage check is its test. Honest and enough. My own read of ON Figure 5 (p.5) at higher zoom: about 6 to 7 nA at 25 degC, about 500 nA at 85 degC (x75 to x85) and about 2.2 uA at its right edge, 100 degC, where the record reads 1.5 uA; at 2.2 uA the 100 degC case is 316 times the 25 degC reading, leakage about 160 uA, x7 on trigger current and 64 mV, x3 on VGD. Held at every corner on either reading; the record's stated tolerance of about 1.5 on each reading covers the difference. Not raised. Note the figure's axis is ambient, so 100 degC is beyond TOPR (85 degC) and is the record's bounding case. |

Round 1's findings 1.1, 1.3, 1.4, 1.5 are named by W-1 to W-4 and fall with 2.2, 2.3 and 2.4 above; 1.2 continues as 3.1; 1.6 to 1.10 continue as 2.5 to 2.9 as the closure table of round 2 says; 1.11 to 1.13 were closed there.

### Round 3 counts

88 verdict rows: 40 pass, 37 fail, 11 not applicable, 0 cannot judge. 3 findings: 3.1 raised by FAULT-1 (safety), 3.2 and 3.3 by the free pass (quality). Every other fail row cites an earlier finding (2.2 to 2.9) that this round reads as closed or carried by the owner's acceptances of 2026-11-18, with Closed by still to be written by the designer. The round is not clean: finding 3.1 is open and 3.2 and 3.3 are open, so a further full round follows their closure. No figure in a verdict rests on memory; the figures this round could not source are the surge generator's source impedance and the 1 kV household-appliance level (IEC 61000-4-5 and CISPR 14-2 not read), the entry varistor's clamp above 50 A, the 07D471K's clamp at currents other than 10 A, the opto-triac's turn-on time and the microsecond withstand of the triac and opto-triac, the 1206 resistor's pulse rating, the opto-triac's thermal resistance, any rating of the push-on terminals and joiners and of the TE 63849-1 blade, a discharge level for the connectors, and the stock and price of C10065, C23552 and C49072860; each is recorded as unsourced in the records and none is filled in here.

Page numbers are each document's own printed page: UR 1206 power rating p.4, ratings table, the RCWV formula and the derating curve p.5, item 4.13 p.6; ZH operating temperature p.1 and the 07D471K row p.2; ON safety and insulation ratings p.2, absolute maximum ratings p.3, characteristics p.4, curves p.5, hot-line circuit p.7, ordering and the V option p.9; ST product summary and package drawing p.1, Tables 1 to 3 p.2, Tables 4 and 5 p.3, Figure 3 p.4, Figure 9 p.5, Tables 8 and 9 pp.15 and 16.
- **Citation key**: ON, ST, UR, ZH, XF and TE as above; circuit-proof.md rows are cited as the record labels them.
- **Reviewer**: one reviewer, a Claude Sonnet 5.5 session that did not draw the schematic and did not judge rounds 1 to 3, working from the commit, the netlist, the drawings (all five sheets rendered with kicad-cli sch export svg and rsvg-convert and read as images), the records and the datasheets above; the fanned-out form was not used.
- **Owner acceptances read**: owner-sign-off.md W-1 to W-4 (David Baker, in his words 'I'm happy with these and sign as accepted', accepted_on 2026-11-18); W-5a to W-5c and W-6 (accepted_by Claude, on behalf of David Baker under his standing instruction of 2026-11-07, accepted_on 2026-11-18, not his words). Each is read against the finding it names. Known and not raised: the blank title blocks (as on the main board's sheets), the mirrored opto-triac drawing, the layout-time hedges of layout-hedges.md, the stock and price of C10065, C23552 and C49072860, and the wrong page citations that remain inside rounds 1 to 3.

### Verdicts

| Item | Net or part | Verdict | Rests on | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PINV-1 | /+5V_WB | pass | netlist J931.1, U911.1, U921.1; onsemi MOC3083M/D Rev. 2 (Sept 2022) p.3 (IF 60 mA, VR 6 V), p.4 (VF 1.5 V max at 30 mA); pin-limits.yaml supply5_wb (5.25 V); circuit-proof.md row (a), circuit-proof/W_margins.log (a) (re-run, output identical to the log) | Supply from the main board, 4.75 to 5.25 V, never below 0 V, against the LED's VR 6 V; the LED current is set by the main board's 180 ohm R763/R773 (5.25 V / (180 ohm x 0.99) = 29.5 mA against IF 60 mA, x2.0). J931 is passive. |
| PINV-1 | /Coffee heater switch/HEAT_C_OPTO_MT | fail | netlist R911.1, U911.6; onsemi MOC3083M/D Rev. 2 (Sept 2022) p.3 (ITSM 1 A single cycle 60 Hz, VDRM 800 V; no pulse withstand stated); circuit-proof.md rows (b) and (d), circuit-proof/W_margins.log (b) (re-run, identical); UNI-ROYAL Thick Film Chip Resistors V.3 (12 Feb 2019) p.5 | 4.2 (new). In service 357.8 V / 396 ohm (two 200 ohm at -1 %) = 0.904 A against ITSM 1 A (x1.11, re-derived). But this net's row in round 3, and row (d) of the record, say the 1.96 A (775 V / 396 ohm) 'does not arise' because the 775 V clamp stands under 800 V, while row (d) in the same cell keeps a dv/dt turn-on of the opto-triac in a surge, which puts that 1.96 A through pin 6 until the triac latches; ON p.3 gives ITSM only as a 60 Hz single cycle, so the microsecond withstand is unsourced and the record contradicts itself. |
| PINV-1 | /Coffee heater switch/HEAT_C_GATE_R | fail | netlist R911.2, R912.1; UNI-ROYAL Thick Film Chip Resistors V.3 (12 Feb 2019) p.5 (ratings table 1206: max working voltage 200 V, max overload voltage 400 V; and the rated-continuous-working-voltage formula RCWV = sqrt(P x R), 'in no case ... greater than the applicable maximum value', overload voltage 2.5 x RCWV or the maximum whichever is less), p.4 (1206 1/4 W at 70 degC); onsemi MOC3083M/D Rev. 2 (Sept 2022) p.3; pin-limits.yaml mains_gate_c, mains_gate_s; circuit-proof.md rows (b), (b2), (d) | 4.2 (new). Midpoint of two equal 200 ohm resistors: 178.9 V peak is half of 357.8 V (re-derived) and the pin-limit record and the check compare it with the 200 V of the rating field. That 200 V is the sheet's cap, not the rating of a 200 ohm part: RCWV = sqrt(0.25 W x 200 ohm) = 7.07 V and the overload voltage 2.5 x 7.07 = 17.7 V (p.5). 178.9 V is 25 times RCWV and 10 times the overload voltage, and in the surge case row (d) keeps (775 V across the pair) each resistor sees 387 V, 55 times RCWV, 22 times the overload voltage and 97 % of the sheet's 400 V maximum. The sheet states no pulse rating (recorded, known), so the pass rests on a rating the sheet does not give. |
| PINV-1 | /Coffee heater switch/HEAT_C_TRIAC_G | pass | netlist R912.2, T911.3; ST DS2114 Rev 11 (May 2018) Table 1 p.2 (IGM 4 A at 20 us, PG(AV) 1 W; no gate-voltage maximum stated), Table 4 p.3 (IGT 50 mA, VGT 1.3 V max); circuit-proof.md row (b) | The gate is rated in current: 0.904 A in service, 1.96 A only if the opto-triac were to break over at the 775 V clamp, both under IGM 4 A. The sheet states no gate voltage maximum and the record does not invent one (pin-limits.yaml carries the gate net as a domain of about 1.3 V over MT1). |

The clamp, 775 V at the sheet's 10 A point, stands 25 V (3.1 %) under both 800 V parts. What current the clamp passes in a surge is judged under SURGE-1, not here. |

| TOUCH-1 | none | not applicable | netlist | No filter or supply capacitor connects a mains conductor to protective earth or to an accessible conductive part: the only parts on mains nets are the opto-triac output, triac, varistor and gate resistors, all between mains conductors. |
| INDEP-1 | none | not applicable | netlist (16 nets, 14 parts: no oscillator, timer, latch, reset net or reset output); circuit-proof.md 'Independence of the heater chain from the controller'; main-board circuit-verdicts.md (Whole board and Interlock rows, INDEP-1 PASS) and main-board schematic-review.md round 7 INDEP-1 rows for the K201 element; onsemi MOC3083M/D Rev. 2 (Sept 2022) p.1 Figure 1, p.4 (IFT 5 mA, IDRM1 500 nA) | Applies when the schematic carries an element that removes power from a hazardous load when the controller stops showing it is running. This board holds no watchdog-held switch, timer or latch; that element is the main board's K201 chain, judged on that board. Traced anyway, for the board's own chain (U911/U921, R911/R912, R921/R922, T911/T921), the three things the item names, separately. CLOCK: no net of the board reaches a clock, oscillator or timer output; the only controller signal is the command on /HEAT_C_LED_K and /HEAT_S_LED_K; with the controller's clock stopped, nothing on this board changes the command and no record shows the main board's sink gate returning to off, so a heater commanded on is not shown to be removed: the chain's default is not shown to be 'removes power' for a stopped clock, which is why it is not the element and why K201 is. RESET: no reset net or output; with the controller in reset the LED current is at most 5 uA (main board row, cited in circuit-proof.md row (c)) against IFT 5 mA max, so both triacs are held off, the direction that removes power. SUPPLY: the only supply is /+5V_WB, the main board's +5V through JP761, the rail the main board's records call the logic supply; below the LED's threshold the LED is unlit and the triac holds off (row (c), x4 to x13 on VGD, an estimate from typical curves); no logic runs on this board. A stuck-on cause begins on this board (FAULT-1), not in the clock, reset or supply. |

### Findings

| Finding | Raised by | Severity | What and where | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4.1 | FAULT-1 | safety | Failure-mode table of circuit-proof.md, row 'LED return (HEAT_C_LED_K, HEAT_S_LED_K)', mode 'shorted to ground on the cable', and W-6 of owner-sign-off.md, which accepts the outcome on that row. The row says the LED is lit 'through the main board's 180 ohm only (about 21 mA)'. The netlist, the main board and this record's own row (c) say otherwise: R763 and R773 sit between J761 pins 2 and 3 and the sink drains (main-board sheets/720 yaml: HEAT_C_LED_K is J761.2 and R763.1, HEAT_C_SINK is R763.2 and Q761.3), so a cable conductor shorted to ground bypasses both the resistor and the sink, and the main board's circuit-proof.md says so (LED sees (5 V - VF) / cable resistance, no resistor in series, R763 on the far side of the short). The LED current is then limited by no part of either board (IF maximum 60 mA, ON p.3), the record gives no figure, and it states neither what the LED does (the likely failed-open or failed-short LED) nor what the load on +5V_WB, JP761 and the 5 V rail does to the controller or to K201, so the safety acceptance W-6 rests on a figure that is wrong by the topology. 21 mA appears nowhere else in the folder. Where: circuit-proof.md failure-mode table (LED return row); owner-sign-off.md W-6; U911, U921, J931. | overridden by: REQ-SAFETY-CHAIN-001.C8 (the LED return shorted on the cable leaves the heater on with its hazard bounded by the element's own thermostats and cutoffs, independent of the failed part and of the controller; the LED's own overstress below 54 ohm does not change that outcome whichever way the LED fails; DEC-CHAIN-FAULT-BOUNDED-BY-INDEPENDENT-ELEMENT addresses the criterion); accepted_by: David Baker (owner-sign-off.md W-6 and W-7(b), in his words "Accept 1 2 and 3"); accepted_on: 2026-11-19; fixed: records corrected 2026-11-18, circuit-proof.md failure-mode table row "LED return" and the section "LED return shorted on the cable", owner-sign-off.md W-6 amended with the corrected figures, open-items.md "LED return shorted on the cable" |
| 4.2 | PINV-1, DERATE-2 | function | Gate resistors R911, R912, R921, R922 (nets HEAT_C_GATE_R, HEAT_S_GATE_R; HEAT_C_OPTO_MT, HEAT_S_OPTO_MT). The records pass the resistors on '178.9 V against 200 V working (x1.12) and 400 V overload' (circuit-proof.md rows (b) and the grading table, pin-limits.yaml, derating-policy.yaml, parts-specification.yaml rating field). UNI-ROYAL V.3 p.5 gives 200 V and 400 V as the maxima and defines the rated working voltage of a part as RCWV = sqrt(P x R), never above the maximum, with the overload voltage 2.5 x RCWV or the maximum, whichever is less: for 200 ohm at 1/4 W (p.4) RCWV is 7.07 V and the overload voltage 17.7 V. The firing pulse (10 V per resistor, 40 us, 2 mW average) is inside those, re-derived; the stress the records test (178.9 V per resistor in the surge case, up to 387 V each with the 775 V clamp across the pair, row (d)) is 10 to 22 times the overload voltage and the sheet states no pulse rating. In the same cell row (d) says the opto-triac's 1.96 A 'does not arise' and then keeps a dv/dt turn-on that puts 1.96 A through pin 6 (ITSM 1 A is a 60 Hz single cycle, ON p.3). The same reading stands for the main board's R704, R705, R714, R715 (circuit-proof.md row (b) says so). Where: circuit-proof.md row (b) and grading table, pin-limits.yaml mains_gate_c and mains_gate_s, derating-policy.yaml resistor, parts-specification.yaml, open-items.md. | overridden by: DEL-HW-HEATER-WALL-BOARD-DESIGNED.C23 (a criterion of the board's own: the resistor's rating covers its stress or the residual is recorded and accepted); accepted_by: David Baker; accepted_on: 2026-11-19 (owner-sign-off.md W-7(a), in his words "Accept 1 2 and 3"); fixed: the drawn Rating field reads "1% 250mW RCWV 7.07V 1206" (commit 0e937fdc), the stress and the missing pulse and voltage rating recorded with the accepted exceptions the pre-order check reads in derating-policy.yaml accepted_exceptions; the larger-resistor hedge is kept in open-items.md |

### Round 3 findings, closure

Each of round 3's findings read against the records and the schematic at c8d1e530, and against the documents re-opened this round. The designer writes Closed by; this table says what it can validly carry. A safety finding closes only by a change or a named person's acceptance (step 5).

| Round 3 finding | Closure verdict at c8d1e530 |
|---|---|
| 3.1 FAULT-1 (the acceptance missing for every outcome that leaves a heater on; the 2.0 A threshold in no node; the third outcome uncited) | **Open, continued.** Done and checked: the failure-mode table names every part and mode and how and when each is detected, with 'not stated' where a record states nothing; DEC-CHAIN-FAULT-BOUNDED-BY-INDEPENDENT-ELEMENT is cited and read (it names the 110 degC coffee and 200 degC steam thermostats, says the reference machine does not establish that they reset, and says a non-restoring device is owed and not fitted: W-6 says the same); OBL-CONTROLLER-FAULT-RESPONSE-001.C2 and DEL-HW-BRING-UP.C23 read, and W-6's 40 times (2.0 A / 50 mA) is the arithmetic and is right; C23's threshold and the 50 mA resolution are as the record states, and the record says plainly that the check of REQ-ACTUATION-CONFIRMATION-001.C6 and the in-service detection are delivered by no node. Not closed, for three reasons. (1) W-6's accepted_by is 'Claude, on behalf of David Baker', and step 5 says a safety finding closes by a named person's acceptance, 'never by the agent that drew or reviewed the board', and 'the name is a person's, never an agent session's'. SOL-HW-HEATER-WALL-BOARD-PROVEN-AND-LAID-OUT.C15 does allow entries 'signed on the owner's behalf' to be marked as such, but the method's step 5 for safety severity does not, and the standing instruction of 2026-11-07 that W-6 cites stops 'for a real safety or performance impact', which a heater left on is; W-6 itself marks 'Read it first'. The cheapest closure is David's own acceptance of W-6 (`accepted_by: David Baker`, with his date), or a decision node of his as DEC-MAIN-BOARD-RESIDUAL-RISKS-ACCEPTED-BY-OWNER is for the main board; a closure by acceptance needs no further schematic round. (2) W-6 accepts 'the LED return shorted to ground on the cable' on a table row that misstates its effect (4.1). (3) The 2.0 A threshold that W-3 and W-6 rely on is still in no graph node (searched again at c8d1e530: nothing carries it), so a load-bearing figure is stated in prose across four records and required by no criterion; the graph, not the records, is the durable place (CLAUDE.md), and the main session still has to author it. |
| 3.2 free pass (stale symbol-library text in the cache and the netlist) | **Closed in substance.** Re-verified at c8d1e530: the netlist still writes the stock text (T911 and T921 libsource description '...600V...', lines 583 and 1063; the MOC3083M symbol's cached Datasheet is the old address), while each instance's own Description says 800 V and its Datasheet is the Rev. 2 address, as the schematic diff from 8463c446 (two fields per sheet) shows. The records now say what the stock-symbol text is and that the check compares the instance fields (parts-specification.yaml header, open-items.md 'Stock library symbols in the cache', circuit-verdicts.md 'Library symbol names'), which is a true statement and enough for a quality finding. Closed by should read `met:` (the fitted part is carried by the instance fields); 'fixed (recorded reason...)' is not one of step 5's forms. Not re-raised. |
| 3.3 free pass (wrong datasheet page citations) | **Closed.** Each corrected citation opened and checked: Zhuhai Hongjiacheng 07D Rev 1.2, electrical table with the 07D471K row (300 VAC, 385 VDC, V1mA 470 V (423 to 517), 775 V at 10 A, 1200 A, 29 J, 0.25 W) p.2, operating temperature -40 to +105 degC p.1; UNI-ROYAL V.3, ratings table p.5, 1/4 W at 70 degC p.4, derating curve p.5, item 4.13 short-time overload p.6; the corrected records are circuit-proof.md, parts-specification.yaml, open-items.md and owner-sign-off.md W-1, and no page citation in them now opens the wrong page. The wrong pages that remain inside rounds 1 to 3 are history and are not raised. Note for finding 4.2: the page is right; what the records read on it (the 200 V maximum as the rated working voltage of a 200 ohm part) is not. |

### Round 4 counts

92 verdict rows: 35 pass, 46 fail, 11 not applicable, 0 cannot judge. 2 new findings: 4.1 raised by FAULT-1 (safety), 4.2 raised by PINV-1 and DERATE-2 (function). Open from earlier rounds: 3.1 (safety; its Closed by is not one step 5 allows, see 'Round 3 findings, closure'); 3.2 and 3.3 are closed. Every other fail row cites an earlier finding (2.2 to 2.9) that earlier rounds read as closed by the owner's own acceptances (W-1 to W-4) or carried by owner-held records (W-5a to W-5c, finding 2.8's bring-up row), with Closed by still to be written by the designer. **Round 4 is not clean**: it raises two findings and finding 3.1 stays open, so it is not the closing round; 4.1 and 4.2 are closed only by a change to the schematic or records (4.2 may need a part or a recorded reason), a change needs a further full round, and 3.1 closes by David's own acceptance without one. No figure in a verdict rests on memory. Re-derived this round from the datasheets and the schematic: 357.8 / 396 = 0.904 A against ITSM 1 A; 357.8 / 198 = 1.81 A for one of the pair shorted; 24.5 V = 0.05 A x 404 ohm + 3.0 V + 1.3 V and 24.5 / 52.7 = 0.465 A against IL 70 mA (x6.6); 500 nA x 404 ohm = 0.2 mV against VGD 0.2 V (x990) and 2.5 mA / 500 nA = x5000, hot x30 and x13 as the record scales them; 253^2 / (4 x 47) = 340.5 W, 2.69 A; 775 V / 1.2 us = 646 V/us against 600 V/us; 253^2 / 47 x 10 ms = 13.6 J; 2.0 A / 50 mA = 40; 2 pi x 60 Hz x 357.8 V = 0.135 V/us and 2 pi x 60 Hz x 5.38 A x sqrt 2 = 2.87 A/ms against 1000 V/us and 14 A/ms; LED 17.88 mA minimum and 29.46 mA maximum; emitter power 56.6 mW at 70 degC and 35.4 mW at 85 degC against 120 mW less 1.41 mW/degC; 59 mA on J931 pin 1 against 3 A; 7.07 V RCWV and 17.7 V overload for a 200 ohm 1/4 W resistor. The figures this round could not source are the surge generator's source impedance and the 1 kV household-appliance level (IEC 61000-4-5 and CISPR 14-2 not read), the entry varistor's clamp above 50 A, the 07D471K's clamp at currents other than 10 A, the opto-triac's turn-on time and the microsecond withstand of the triac, the opto-triac and the 1206 resistors, the 1206 resistor's pulse rating (the sheet states none), the time the gate resistors take to open, the opto-triac's thermal resistance, whether the elements' thermostats reset, the K201 coil inductance beyond the main board's 4.73 H bound, any rating of the push-on terminals and joiners and of the TE 63849-1 blade (TE application specification 114-2115 not obtained), a discharge level for the connectors, the cable resistance in finding 4.1 and so the LED's current in that fault, and the stock and price of C10065, C23552 and C49072860; the typical-curve readings of ON Figures 3 and 5 and ST Figure 9 that the hold-off arithmetic rests on were not re-read this round (ST Figure 3 was). Each is recorded as unsourced in the records and none is filled in here.

In service 357.8 V / 396 ohm (two 200 ohm at -1 %) = 0.904 A against ITSM 1 A (x1.11, re-derived). But this net's row in round 3, and row (d) of the record, say the 1.96 A (775 V / 396 ohm) 'does not arise' because the 775 V clamp stands under 800 V, while row (d) in the same cell keeps a dv/dt turn-on of the opto-triac in a surge, which puts that 1.96 A through pin 6 until the triac latches; ON p.3 gives ITSM only as a 60 Hz single cycle, so the microsecond withstand is unsourced and the record contradicts itself. |
| PINV-1 | /Coffee heater switch/HEAT_C_GATE_R | fail | netlist R911.2, R912.1; UNI-ROYAL Thick Film Chip Resistors V.3 (12 Feb 2019) p.5 (ratings table 1206: max working voltage 200 V, max overload voltage 400 V; and the rated-continuous-working-voltage formula RCWV = sqrt(P x R), 'in no case ... greater than the applicable maximum value', overload voltage 2.5 x RCWV or the maximum whichever is less), p.4 (1206 1/4 W at 70 degC); onsemi MOC3083M/D Rev. 2 (Sept 2022) p.3; pin-limits.yaml mains_gate_c, mains_gate_s; circuit-proof.md rows (b), (b2), (d) | 4.2 (an earlier-round finding, closed by the owner's recorded reason W-7(a), accepted_by David Baker, accepted_on 2026-11-19; the resistors' rated voltage and pulse stay unsourced, re-derived here: RCWV = sqrt(0.25 W x 200 ohm) = 7.07 V and overload voltage 2.5 x 7.07 = 17.7 V, UR p.5; the records no longer pass the resistors on the 200 V maximum). Midpoint of two equal 200 ohm resistors: 178.9 V peak is half of 357.8 V (re-derived) and the pin-limit record and the check compare it with the 200 V of the rating field. That 200 V is the sheet's cap, not the rating of a 200 ohm part: RCWV = sqrt(0.25 W x 200 ohm) = 7.07 V and the overload voltage 2.5 x 7.07 = 17.7 V (p.5). 178.9 V is 25 times RCWV and 10 times the overload voltage, and in the surge case row (d) keeps (775 V across the pair) each resistor sees 387 V, 55 times RCWV, 22 times the overload voltage and 97 % of the sheet's 400 V maximum. Midpoint of two equal 200 ohm resistors: 178.9 V peak is half of 357.8 V (re-derived) and the pin-limit record and the check compare it with the 200 V of the rating field. That 200 V is the sheet's cap, not the rating of a 200 ohm part: RCWV = sqrt(0.25 W x 200 ohm) = 7.07 V and the overload voltage 2.5 x 7.07 = 17.7 V (p.5). 178.9 V is 25 times RCWV and 10 times the overload voltage, and in the surge case row (d) keeps (775 V across the pair) each resistor sees 387 V, 55 times RCWV, 22 times the overload voltage and 97 % of the sheet's 400 V maximum. The sheet states no pulse rating (recorded, known), so the pass rests on a rating the sheet does not give. |

| TOUCH-1 | none | not applicable | netlist | No filter or supply capacitor connects a mains conductor to protective earth or to an accessible conductive part: the only parts on mains nets are the opto-triac output, triac, varistor and gate resistors, all between mains conductors. |
| INDEP-1 | none | not applicable | netlist (16 nets, 14 parts: no oscillator, timer, latch, reset net or reset output); circuit-proof.md 'Independence of the heater chain from the controller'; main-board circuit-verdicts.md (Whole board and Interlock rows, INDEP-1 PASS) and main-board schematic-review.md round 7 INDEP-1 rows for the K201 element; onsemi MOC3083M/D Rev. 2 (Sept 2022) p.1 Figure 1, p.4 (IFT 5 mA, IDRM1 500 nA) | Applies when the schematic carries an element that removes power from a hazardous load when the controller stops showing it is running. This board holds no watchdog-held switch, timer or latch; that element is the main board's K201 chain, judged on that board. Traced anyway, for the board's own chain (U911/U921, R911/R912, R921/R922, T911/T921), the three things the item names, separately. CLOCK: no net of the board reaches a clock, oscillator or timer output; the only controller signal is the command on /HEAT_C_LED_K and /HEAT_S_LED_K; with the controller's clock stopped, nothing on this board changes the command and no record shows the main board's sink gate returning to off, so a heater commanded on is not shown to be removed: the chain's default is not shown to be 'removes power' for a stopped clock, which is why it is not the element and why K201 is. RESET: no reset net or output; with the controller in reset the LED current is at most 5 uA (main board row, cited in circuit-proof.md row (c)) against IFT 5 mA max, so both triacs are held off, the direction that removes power. SUPPLY: the only supply is /+5V_WB, the main board's +5V through JP761, the rail the main board's records call the logic supply; below the LED's threshold the LED is unlit and the triac holds off (row (c), x4 to x13 on VGD, an estimate from typical curves); no logic runs on this board. A stuck-on cause begins on this board (FAULT-1), not in the clock, reset or supply. |

### Findings

| Finding | Raised by | Severity | What and where | Closed by |

Each of round 4's findings, and each earlier finding whose Closed by is a safety acceptance, read against the records at 339bd1fa and the documents re-opened this round. The designer writes Closed by; this table says what it can validly carry. A safety finding closes only by a change or a named person's acceptance of a reason (step 5), and the review closes only on a clean full round in which every finding of every round is closed.

### Round 5 counts

92 verdict rows: 35 pass, 46 fail, 11 not applicable, 0 cannot judge. 1 new finding: 5.1 raised by the free pass (quality). Every other fail row cites an earlier finding (2.2 to 2.9, 3.1, 4.1, 4.2). **Round 5 is not clean.** Reasons: (1) finding 5.1 is open, and the change it asks for (amend six statements in the records) is to records, not the schematic, but it needs a further full round to close under step 6 because a round after a change to the records is judged as a new full round; (2) the Closed by cells of 1.2, 2.1, 3.1, 4.1 and 4.2 do not yet say what the owner's acceptances now allow them to say (agent names on safety findings in three, 'not closed' wording and no step 5 form in two); (3) 2.7 is a recorded negative current margin carried without a step 5 form. The schematic itself is unchanged and no new circuit defect was found: every net and pin agrees with schematic-spec.yaml, the ON pinout (p.1: 1 anode, 2 cathode, 3 N/C, 4 and 6 main terminals, 5 NC substrate) and, for the ST triac, the A1, A2, G order as schematic-spec.yaml records it from the package picture, which this round did not re-read and the main board's J761; ERC reports 0 violations; every field of every part agrees with parts-specification.yaml (reference, value, package, rating, LCSC); the drawn sheets are legible, with nothing overlapping. What would make a next round clean: the records and the Closed by cells amended as above, 2.7 given a form or David's own words, and nothing in the schematic changed. No figure in a verdict rests on memory. Not sourced by this round and recorded as unsourced in the records, none filled in here: the surge generator's source impedance and the household-appliance surge level (IEC 61000-4-5 and CISPR 14-2 not read), the entry varistor's clamp above 50 A, the 07D471K's clamp at currents other than 10 A, the opto-triac's turn-on time and the microsecond withstand of the triac, the opto-triac and the 1206 resistors, the 1206 resistor's pulse rating, the time the gate resistors take to open, the opto-triac's thermal resistance, whether the elements' thermostats reset, any rating of the push-on terminals and joiners and of the TE 63849-1 blade, a discharge level for the connectors, the cable resistance in finding 4.1 and so the LED's current in that fault, what an over-current LED does, and the stock and price of C10065, C23552 and C49072860.

Also 6.1: the drawn Rating field of this part still reads '1% 250mW 200V 1206'. Also 6.1 (R911.1 or R921.1 is on this net). |
| PINV-1 | /Steam heater switch/HEAT_S_GATE_R | fail | netlist R921.2, R922.1; UNI-ROYAL Thick Film Chip Resistors V.3 (12 Feb 2019) p.5 (ratings table 1206: max working voltage 200 V, max overload voltage 400 V; and the rated-continuous-working-voltage formula RCWV = sqrt(P x R), 'in no case ... greater than the applicable maximum value', overload voltage 2.5 x RCWV or the maximum whichever is less), p.4 (1206 1/4 W at 70 degC); onsemi MOC3083M/D Rev. 2 (Sept 2022) p.3; pin-limits.yaml mains_gate_c, mains_gate_s; circuit-proof.md rows (b), (b2), (d) | 4.2 (earlier finding; the owner's acceptance W-7(a), accepted_by David Baker, accepted_on 2026-11-19, its Closed by names the owner's record and not a requirement, see 'Earlier findings, closure'; the resistors' rated voltage and pulse stay unsourced, re-derived here: RCWV = sqrt(0.25 W x 200 ohm) = 7.07 V and overload voltage 2.5 x 7.07 = 17.7 V, UR p.5; the records no longer pass the resistors on the 200 V maximum). Midpoint of two equal 200 ohm resistors: 178.9 V peak is half of 357.8 V (re-derived) and the pin-limit record and the check compare it with the 200 V of the rating field. That 200 V is the sheet's cap, not the rating of a 200 ohm part: RCWV = sqrt(0.25 W x 200 ohm) = 7.07 V and the overload voltage 2.5 x 7.07 = 17.7 V (p.5). 178.9 V is 25 times RCWV and 10 times the overload voltage, and in the surge case row (d) keeps (775 V across the pair) each resistor sees 387 V, 55 times RCWV, 22 times the overload voltage and 97 % of the sheet's 400 V maximum. The sheet states no pulse rating (recorded, known), so the pass rests on a rating the sheet does not give. Also 6.1: the drawn Rating field of this part still reads '1% 250mW 200V 1206'. |

| TOUCH-1 | none | not applicable | netlist | No filter or supply capacitor connects a mains conductor to protective earth or to an accessible conductive part: the only parts on mains nets are the opto-triac output, triac, varistor and gate resistors, all between mains conductors. |
| INDEP-1 | none | not applicable | netlist (16 nets, 14 parts: no oscillator, timer, latch, reset net or reset output); circuit-proof.md 'Independence of the heater chain from the controller'; main-board circuit-verdicts.md (Whole board and Interlock rows, INDEP-1 PASS) and main-board schematic-review.md round 7 INDEP-1 rows for the K201 element; onsemi MOC3083M/D Rev. 2 (Sept 2022) p.1 Figure 1, p.4 (IFT 5 mA, IDRM1 500 nA) | Applies when the schematic carries an element that removes power from a hazardous load when the controller stops showing it is running. This board holds no watchdog-held switch, timer or latch; that element is the main board's K201 chain, judged on that board. Traced anyway, for the board's own chain (U911/U921, R911/R912, R921/R922, T911/T921), the three things the item names, separately. CLOCK: no net of the board reaches a clock, oscillator or timer output; the only controller signal is the command on /HEAT_C_LED_K and /HEAT_S_LED_K; with the controller's clock stopped, nothing on this board changes the command and no record shows the main board's sink gate returning to off, so a heater commanded on is not shown to be removed: the chain's default is not shown to be 'removes power' for a stopped clock, which is why it is not the element and why K201 is. RESET: no reset net or output; with the controller in reset the LED current is at most 5 uA (main board row, cited in circuit-proof.md row (c)) against IFT 5 mA max, so both triacs are held off, the direction that removes power. SUPPLY: the only supply is /+5V_WB, the main board's +5V through JP761, the rail the main board's records call the logic supply; below the LED's threshold the LED is unlit and the triac holds off (row (c), x4 to x13 on VGD, an estimate from typical curves); no logic runs on this board. A stuck-on cause begins on this board (FAULT-1), not in the clock, reset or supply. |

### Earlier findings, closure

Each earlier finding, read against the records and documents at 86fdfa1b. The designer writes Closed by; this table says what each can validly carry under step 5: a change naming its commit, or a reason written `met:`, `does not apply:` or `overridden by:` naming a requirement the board serves or a catalogue item; for a safety finding a reason closes only with a named person's `accepted_by:` and `accepted_on:` in the cell. The main board's own review (schematic-review.md lines 3377 to 3391) held that a decision is neither a requirement nor a catalogue item, that a recorded acceptance of a shortfall is not a reason the item is met, and that a decision closes a finding only through a requirement it addresses; the wall board's records are held to the same reading.

### Round 6 counts

92 verdict rows: 35 pass, 46 fail, 11 not applicable, 0 cannot judge. 2 new findings, both raised by the free pass and both of quality severity: 6.1 (the gate resistors' drawn Rating field) and 6.2 (the 2.0 A stuck-on threshold in no graph node). Every other fail row cites an earlier finding (2.2 to 2.9, 3.1, 4.1, 4.2). **Round 6 is not clean.** Reasons: (1) 6.1 and 6.2 are open; (2) of the earlier findings only 5.1, 1.1, 1.13, 2.10, 2.11, 3.2, 3.3 are validly closed; the Closed by of 1.2, 2.1, 3.1 and 4.1 need their requirement and keys written (wording), 1.11 and 1.12 need their reasons rewritten (wording), 1.3, 1.4, 2.2, 2.3, 1.5, 2.4, 2.5, 2.6, 2.7, 2.8, 2.9 and 4.2 have no step 5 form yet and need a record change, a schematic change or an owner decision as the table says. The schematic is unchanged since round 4 and no new circuit defect was found: every net and pin agrees with schematic-spec.yaml and the ON pinout, ERC is clean, the render is legible with nothing overlapping, and the key arithmetic re-derives. What would make a next round clean: 6.1 changed (a schematic change), 6.2's node authored, the Closed by cells rewritten as above, the constraints-record rows added or the carry route taken, and the owner's decisions on the barrier entries and the varistor's series device. Not sourced by this round and recorded as unsourced in the records, none filled in here: the surge generator's source impedance and the household-appliance surge level, the entry varistor's clamp above 50 A, the 07D471K's clamp at currents other than 10 A, the microsecond withstand of the triac, the opto-triac and the 1206 resistors, the 1206 resistor's pulse rating, the opto-triac's thermal resistance, whether the elements' thermostats reset, any rating of the push-on terminals and joiners and of the TE 63849-1 blade, a discharge level for the connectors, the cable resistance in finding 4.1 and what an over-current LED does.

UNI-ROYAL Thick Film Chip Resistors, Part Name 01005 to 2512 Series, V.3, Feb. 12, 2019, https://datasheet.lcsc.com/datasheet/pdf/0a975aaa49b7c97f38a963127be4a823.pdf, 9 pages: the ratings table (1206: max working voltage 200 V, max overload voltage 400 V, dielectric withstanding 500 V) on the page footed 'Page 5/9' and RCWV = sqrt(P x R), overload 2.5 x RCWV or the maximum, whichever is less. Not re-opened this round: the Zhuhai Hongjiacheng 07D sheet, the TE drawing, ST Fig. 3 and ON Figs. 3 and 5 (their figures carry round 4 to 6's reading).
- **Arithmetic re-derived this round**: 253 x sqrt 2 = 357.8 V; 357.8 / 396 = 0.904 A against ITSM 1 A (x1.11); RCWV = sqrt(0.25 x 200) = 7.07 V and overload voltage 2.5 x 7.07 = 17.7 V (under the 200 V and 400 V maxima, so they govern); 0.05 x 404 + 3.0 + 1.3 = 24.5 V, 24.5 / 52.7 = 0.465 A against IL 70 mA (x6.6); partly failed varistor fed through a 47 ohm element: I = 253 / 94 = 2.69 A, varistor dissipation I^2 x 47 = 253^2 / (4 x 47) = 340.5 W in the varistor alone and as much again in the element (681 W together); LED return short (4.75 - 1.5) / 0.060 = 54.2 ohm, (5.25 - 1.5) / 0.060 = 62.5 ohm, emitter 120 - 1.41 x 45 = 56.6 mW at 70 degC; the 2026-11-19 triac thermal figures from the record's own equation Tj = Ta + P (Rwall + Rbracket + Rfilm + Rc) + 0.5325 P (0.50 + 2.1): 25 + 9.09 x 6.466 + 4.84 x 2.6 = 96.4 degC (25 degC, h 10, Rc 0), 111.4 (40 degC), 123.8 and 138.8 (h 5), steam alone 25 + 4.84 x 9.066 + 12.6 = 68.9 and 83.9 (h 10), 83.5 and 98.5 (h 5), Rc 1 and 3 K/W cells 105.5, 123.6, 120.5, 138.6 (h 10) by adding 9.09 Rc, case = Tj - 4.84 x 2.1 = 86.2 and 101.2 degC, current share 16 x (125 - 86.2) / 39 / 2 = 7.95 A and 4.88 A (chord) at 40 degC; the steam-alone cells above 100 degC are 40 degC at h 5 with Rc 1 and 3 (103.4 and 113.0 degC, the record's, agreeing to rounding). Each agrees with the record and with W_margins.log (e) amendment. Rc, the film coefficients h = 10 and 5, the steel's grade and thickness and the idle-hold power are inputs the records mark unsourced or unmeasured; none is filled in here.
- **Owner acceptances read**: owner-sign-off.md W-1 to W-4 (accepted_by David Baker, in his words "I'm happy with these and sign as accepted", 2026-11-18), W-5c, W-6 and W-7 (accepted_by David Baker, in his words quoted in each, 2026-11-19); W-5a, W-5b and W-5d (accepted_by Claude, on behalf of David Baker, under the standing instruction of 2026-11-07; not his words). This reviewer cannot hear the words; it reads that the record names a person and a date no later than the commit (d9c2c49a, 2026-11-19).
- **Reviewer**: one reviewer, a Claude Sonnet 5.5 session. All five sheets were rendered (`kicad-cli sch export pdf`, `pdftoppm`) and read as images: the root sheet with its four sheet symbols and their pins, the two switch sheets with the opto-triac pins 1 and 2 on the left, the gate chain, triac and varistor, the header J931 with three labelled pins, the return landing J941; no overlapping text and no label over a wire.
